# 1. THE FOUR PILLARS: FOUNDATION OF NET



## 1.1 OVERVIEW



The Four Pillars are the **immutable core** of NET's teaching methodology. Every lesson, every interaction, every pedagogical decision must align with these principles.



### WHY FOUR PILLARS?



Traditional Vietnamese English education fails because it violates natural language acquisition. Students study grammar rules for years but can't speak because they:

- Never listen enough (input deficit)

- Rarely practice speaking (output deficit)

- Learn words in isolation (no context)

- Fear mistakes (confidence deficit)



**NET reverses this entirely.**



---



## 1.2 PILLAR 1: LISTEN & UNDERSTAND



### THE PRINCIPLE

**Students must consume massive amounts of comprehensible input.**



Language is acquired, not learned. The brain needs thousands of hours of exposure to patterns, rhythms, intonation, and vocabulary before it can produce fluent speech.



### THE METHOD

**60% Rule:** Content must be 60-80% comprehensible.

- **Below 60%:** Student is lost, frustrated, overwhelmed (Noise wins)

- **60-80%:** Flow state, optimal learning, brain fills gaps naturally

- **Above 90%:** Too easy, no growth, student gets bored



### IMPLEMENTATION



**DAILY PRACTICE (45 minutes minimum):**

- Watch English content (movies, YouTube, podcasts)

- Choose topics student ENJOYS (critical for engagement)

- Use subtitles strategically:

- Beginners: Vietnamese subtitles okay initially

- Intermediate: English subtitles preferred

- Advanced: No subtitles, pure listening



**IN-CLASS PRACTICE:**

- Teacher speaks 70% in English, 30% Vietnamese (beginners)

- Teacher speaks 90% in English, 10% Vietnamese (intermediate+)

- Use visuals, gestures, context to convey meaning

- Repeat key phrases multiple times in different contexts



**CONTENT TYPES:**

- Fun (top priority): Gaming streams, comedy, music, movies

- Interesting: Documentaries, TED talks, interviews

- Useful: Business podcasts, technical videos, news



**CRITICAL RULE:**

**If the student doesn't enjoy the content, they won't listen consistently. Fun beats perfection.**



---



## 1.3 PILLAR 2: SPEAK & MIMIC



### THE PRINCIPLE

**Output is the engine of fluency. Confidence comes from speaking, not studying.**



Students must speak from Day 1, even if it's broken, slow, or grammatically incorrect.



### THE METHOD

**Mimic-First Approach:**

- Listen to a phrase

- Repeat it exactly (intonation, rhythm, emotion)

- Don't analyze grammar — just copy

- Build muscle memory in mouth and brain



### IMPLEMENTATION



**DAILY PRACTICE (15 minutes minimum):**

- Shadow-speak: Play audio, pause, repeat out loud

- Record self speaking

- Compare recording to native speaker

- Focus on sounds, not perfection



**IN-CLASS PRACTICE:**

- **Choral Repetition:** Entire class repeats phrases together

- **Pair Practice:** Students drill phrases in pairs (Tiger energy!)

- **Solo Performance:** Each student speaks individually (confidence building)

- **No Translation:** Students think in English, not translate from Vietnamese



**PRONUNCIATION FOCUS:**

Use the **42 Jolly Phonics Sounds** (see Section 9) to build accurate pronunciation from the beginning.



**CRITICAL RULE:**

**Volume = Confidence. Tiger Voice is loud. Mouse Voice is quiet. Always encourage louder speech.**



---



## 1.4 PILLAR 3: LEARN IN CONTEXT



### THE PRINCIPLE

**Words in isolation are useless. Real sentences stick.**



Traditional education teaches vocabulary lists. Students memorize words but can't use them in conversation. Why? Because the brain doesn't store isolated data — it stores patterns and associations.



### THE METHOD

**Context-First Teaching:**

- Never teach vocabulary in lists

- Always teach words inside real sentences

- Always show multiple example sentences

- Always connect to student's life or interests



### IMPLEMENTATION



**VOCABULARY TEACHING:**

❌ **WRONG:**

> "Sarcasm means... irony or... mocking tone..."

> Word: Sarcasm

> Translation: Mỉa mai



✅ **CORRECT:**

> "Oh great, another meeting. Just what I needed today."

> [Student hears tone, sees eye roll, feels the sarcasm]

> Teacher explains: "This is sarcasm. I don't actually want a meeting. I'm complaining in a funny way."



**EXAMPLE SENTENCES (Show 3-5 per concept):**

1. "Yeah, sure, I'd love to work overtime." (sarcasm = I don't want to)

2. "Thanks a lot for inviting me." (sarcasm = You didn't invite me, I'm annoyed)

3. "Oh, wonderful. Traffic jam." (sarcasm = This is not wonderful)



**ASSOCIATION STRATEGIES:**

- Connect new words to student's hobbies (gaming, tech, food)

- Use memes, movie quotes, viral videos

- Create emotional associations (funny, shocking, relatable)

- Build word families (sarcasm → sarcastic → sarcastically)



**CRITICAL RULE:**

**If a student can't use the word in a real sentence within 5 minutes of learning it, you taught it wrong.**



---



## 1.5 PILLAR 4: BUILD CONFIDENCE



### THE PRINCIPLE

**Fear is the #1 barrier to fluency. Confidence comes before perfection.**



Vietnamese students are trained to avoid mistakes at all costs. This creates **Mouse Voice** — the quiet, scared, apologizing speaker who never improves because they never practice.



**NET reverses this.**



Mistakes are proof of effort. Errors are data. Speaking badly is better than not speaking at all.



### THE METHOD

**Safe Environment:**

- Celebrate mistakes

- Never shame errors

- Praise courage over accuracy

- Create community of support



### IMPLEMENTATION



**IN-CLASS CULTURE:**

- "Great try!" is more important than "Correct!"

- When student makes mistake: "Good! You spoke! Now let's polish it."

- When student is silent: "I need Tiger Voice. Try again, louder."

- When student apologizes: "Tigers don't apologize for learning."



**BUDDY SYSTEM (see Section 8):**

- Pair students for weekly practice

- Encourage peer support

- Build friendships through learning

- Create accountability partners



**COMMUNITY EVENTS (Monthly):**

- Networking dinners in Saigon

- Group activities (karaoke, game nights, debates)

- Alumni talks (show success stories)

- Create sense of belonging



**FEEDBACK PROTOCOLS (see Section 6.2):**

- **Sandwich Method:** Praise → Correction → Praise

- Example:

1. "Great job speaking for 30 seconds!"

2. "Let's fix this grammar: 'I go' should be 'I went'"

3. "Your confidence is excellent. Keep that energy!"



**CRITICAL RULE:**

**Confidence is built through repetition, encouragement, and small wins. Never criticize harshly. Never compare students negatively.**



---



## 1.6 THE FIVE FORCES (METAPHYSICS OF LEARNING)



These are the invisible dynamics that govern every lesson:



### ENERGY

**Definition:** Student enthusiasm, pace, engagement



**HIGH ENERGY:**

- Students talk over each other (good!)

- Laughter, excitement, movement

- Time flies



**LOW ENERGY:**

- Students silent, checking phones

- Awkward pauses

- Dragging pace



**TEACHER CONTROL:**

- Raise energy: Games, debates, humor

- Lower energy: Reading, writing, reflection



---



### FLOW

**Definition:** Optimal learning state where student is fully immersed



**FLOW INDICATORS:**

- No translation (thinking in English)

- No clock-watching

- Mistakes made without self-consciousness

- Deep focus



**HOW TO CREATE FLOW:**

- Content at 60-80% comprehension

- Clear goals for the session

- Immediate feedback

- Balance of challenge and skill



---



### NOISE

**Definition:** Cognitive overload, the villain of learning



**NOISE HAPPENS WHEN:**

- Too much new information at once

- Grammar rules explained mid-conversation

- Constant interruptions for corrections

- Content too difficult (<60% comprehension)



**HOW TO REDUCE NOISE:**

- Slow down

- Use visuals and gestures

- Simplify sentences

- Focus on meaning, not grammar



---



### CLARITY

**Definition:** Direct understanding without translation



**CLARITY ACHIEVED WHEN:**

- Student understands meaning instantly

- No need to translate to Vietnamese

- Natural comprehension



**HOW TO BUILD CLARITY:**

- Use context clues (pictures, gestures, tone)

- Show, don't explain

- Repeat key phrases in multiple contexts

- Use simple English to explain complex concepts



---



### DRIFT

**Definition:** System deviation from canon



**DRIFT IS THE ENEMY.**



Any lesson, teacher, or content that violates the Four Pillars creates drift. Drift compounds over time and destroys the system.



**ZERO DRIFT PROTOCOL:**

- All lessons audited for Pillar compliance

- Teachers retrained if drift detected

- Content updated to eliminate contradictions



---



# 2. LESSON ARCHITECTURE: THE 10-5-5-3 SYSTEM



## 2.1 OVERVIEW



Every NET lesson follows the **10-5-5-3 Pattern**:

- **10** Core Vocabulary words

- **5** Idioms

- **5** Slang expressions

- **3** Games (Warm-up, Mid-lesson, Final challenge)



This structure is **non-negotiable** for standard 90-minute classes.



---



## 2.2 THE 90-MINUTE BREAKDOWN



### SEGMENT 1: WARM-UP (10 minutes)

**Goal:** Break ice, activate English brain, raise energy



**Activities:**

- "Weekend Stories" round-robin

- Current mood check-in

- Quick vocab review from last session

- High-energy greeting ritual



**Example:**

> Teacher: "Everyone stand up! How was your weekend? Kiet, start! 30 seconds, GO!"



---



### SEGMENT 2: INPUT (5 minutes)

**Goal:** Present new content (vocabulary, grammar pattern, idiom)



**Method:**

- Show, don't lecture

- Use real examples (movie clips, tweets, memes)

- Keep it SHORT (attention span is limited)



**Example (Teaching Sarcasm):**

> Teacher shows clip from The Office where Jim says, "Oh, that's a great idea" with sarcastic tone.

> Teacher: "Notice his face. Notice his voice. This is sarcasm. He doesn't mean it."



---



### SEGMENT 3: GUIDED PRACTICE (5 minutes)

**Goal:** Students try new content with teacher support



**Method:**

- Choral repetition (entire class repeats together)

- Pair drills (students practice with partners)

- Error correction in real-time (gentle, encouraging)



**Example:**

> Teacher: "Everyone repeat after me: 'Oh great, another meeting.' Try to sound annoyed!"

> Class: "Oh great, another meeting!"

> Teacher: "Good! Now in pairs, create your own sarcastic sentence. 2 minutes, GO!"



---



### SEGMENT 4: FREE PRACTICE (60 minutes)

**Goal:** Students speak freely, apply content in real conversations



**Method:**

- Pair/group conversations

- Role-plays, debates, storytelling

- Teacher circulates, listens, takes notes

- **NO interruption during speech** (critical rule)



**Typical Activities:**

- **Role-play:** "You're at a restaurant. The food is terrible. Use sarcasm to complain politely."

- **Debate:** "Which is better: iPhone or Android? Convince your partner."

- **Storytelling:** "Tell a story about a time you used sarcasm and got in trouble."



**TEACHER ROLE:**

- Listen actively

- Note common errors (write on board for later)

- Praise specific wins ("Great use of 'I'd rather'!")

- Don't interrupt unless student is completely stuck



---



### SEGMENT 5: FEEDBACK (10 minutes)

**Goal:** Correct common errors, reinforce wins, recap lesson



**Method:**

- List 3-5 common mistakes on board

- Explain correct versions

- Praise 3 students for specific achievements

- Quick recap of lesson focus



**Example:**

> Teacher: "Great job today! Three things to fix:

> 1. 'I go yesterday' → 'I went yesterday' (past tense)

> 2. 'He don't like' → 'He doesn't like' (third person)

> 3. 'More better' → 'Better' (no 'more' needed)

>

> Three wins:

> - Kiet: Amazing sarcasm in role-play!

> - Nhan: You spoke louder today, great Tiger energy!

> - Quang: Perfect pronunciation of 'throughout'!

>

> Homework: Watch 1 episode of Friends, count how many times they use sarcasm."



---



## 2.3 THE THREE GAMES MANDATE



Every lesson MUST include **3 games**:



### GAME 1: WARM-UP GAME (First 10 minutes)

**Purpose:** Break ice, raise energy, activate brains



**Examples:**

- **Two Truths, One Lie:** Students share 3 statements, class guesses which is false

- **Would You Rather:** "Would you rather fight 100 duck-sized horses or 1 horse-sized duck?"

- **Hot Seat:** One student faces away from board, class describes word on screen without saying it



---



### GAME 2: MID-LESSON GAME (After 45 minutes)

**Purpose:** Re-energize, apply lesson content, make learning fun



**Examples:**

- **Sarcasm Olympics:** Teams compete to deliver most convincing sarcastic lines

- **Debate Championship:** Pair vs pair, 2-minute arguments, class votes on winner

- **Charades:** Act out idioms, phrases, or scenarios



---



### GAME 3: FINAL CHALLENGE (Last 15 minutes)

**Purpose:** High-stakes practice, demonstrate mastery, end on high note



**Examples:**

- **Mock Interview:** Students interview each other for dream job

- **Shark Tank:** Pitch a ridiculous product to the class

- **Improv Scene:** Teacher gives scenario, students act it out using lesson vocab



**CRITICAL RULE:**

**Games are not filler. Games are where real learning happens. Pressure + Fun = Retention.**



---



## 2.4 LESSON TEMPLATE STRUCTURE



All lesson templates follow this format:



```markdown

# LESSON: [TOPIC NAME]

**Level:** Beginner / Intermediate / Advanced

**Duration:** 90 minutes

**Objectives:** [What students will be able to do by end]



## VOCABULARY (10 words)

1. Word 1 — Definition — Example sentence

2. Word 2 — Definition — Example sentence

[...10 total]



## IDIOMS (5 phrases)

1. Idiom 1 — Meaning — Example usage

2. Idiom 2 — Meaning — Example usage

[...5 total]



## SLANG (5 expressions)

1. Slang 1 — Meaning — When to use

2. Slang 2 — Meaning — When to use

[...5 total]



## GAME 1: WARM-UP

[Description, rules, timing]



## INPUT SEGMENT

[New content presentation — 5 min]



## GUIDED PRACTICE

[Drills, choral repetition — 5 min]



## GAME 2: MID-LESSON

[Description, rules, timing]



## FREE PRACTICE ACTIVITIES

[3-5 conversation prompts or scenarios — 60 min total]



## GAME 3: FINAL CHALLENGE

[Description, rules, timing]



## FEEDBACK & RECAP

[Common errors to address, praise points]



## HOMEWORK

[Assignment for daily practice]

```



---



# 3. THE 60 LESSON TOPICS: COMPLETE CURRICULUM



## 3.1 TOPIC CATEGORIES



NET curriculum covers **60 core topics** organized into 8 categories:



1. **Pragmatics** (Cultural Communication) — 12 topics

2. **Workplace English** (Professional) — 12 topics

3. **Social Situations** (Everyday Life) — 10 topics

4. **Tech & Gaming** (Modern Slang) — 8 topics

5. **Emotions & Psychology** (Inner World) — 6 topics

6. **Advanced Grammar** (Complex Patterns) — 6 topics

7. **Pronunciation Drills** (Accent Reduction) — 4 topics

8. **Business English** (Corporate) — 2 topics



---



## 3.2 CATEGORY 1: PRAGMATICS (CULTURAL COMMUNICATION)



**Purpose:** Teach cultural nuances that Vietnamese students miss in traditional education



### TOPIC 1: SARCASM

**Lesson Focus:** "Yeah, right" / "Good luck with that" / "Thanks a lot"



**Key Learning:**

- Tone and facial expression matter more than words

- Sarcasm is common in English-speaking cultures

- How to detect sarcasm (context clues)

- How to use sarcasm politely



**Example Sentences:**

- "Oh great, another Zoom meeting." (sarcasm = I don't want this)

- "Sure, I'd love to work on Saturday." (sarcasm = No, I wouldn't)



---



### TOPIC 2: FUNNY VS. FUNNY

**Lesson Focus:** Different types of humor



**Key Learning:**

- "Funny ha-ha" (makes you laugh) vs. "Funny weird" (strange, odd)

- Slapstick vs. dry humor vs. dark humor

- Cultural differences in comedy



**Example Sentences:**

- "That movie was funny!" (ha-ha)

- "That smell is funny..." (weird)



---



### TOPIC 3: UNDERSTATEMENT

**Lesson Focus:** British/American tendency to downplay



**Key Learning:**

- "Not bad" = actually quite good

- "A bit annoying" = extremely annoying

- Why English speakers don't exaggerate emotions



**Example Sentences:**

- "The weather is a bit chilly." (actually freezing)

- "I'm somewhat disappointed." (actually devastated)



---



### TOPIC 4: READING BETWEEN THE LINES

**Lesson Focus:** Indirect communication



**Key Learning:**

- What people mean vs. what they say

- "We should hang out sometime" (often meaningless politeness)

- "Let me think about it" (usually means no)



**Example Sentences:**

- "That's interesting..." (I disagree but won't say it)

- "I'll try my best" (I probably can't do it)



---



### TOPIC 5: VAGUE TIME

**Lesson Focus:** "Soon" / "Later" / "Eventually"



**Key Learning:**

- English time words are imprecise

- "Soon" could mean 5 minutes or 5 days

- Cultural frustration with vague timelines



**Example Sentences:**

- "I'll do it later" (no specific time promised)

- "We'll get there eventually" (could be hours)



---



### TOPIC 6: PASSIVE AGGRESSION

**Lesson Focus:** Indirect anger



**Key Learning:**

- Saying one thing, meaning another (with anger)

- "Fine." (not fine at all)

- How to recognize and respond to passive aggression



**Example Sentences:**

- "No, really, it's fine. Don't worry about it." (angry)

- "Whatever you want." (I don't agree but won't fight)



---



### TOPIC 7: "INTERESTING"

**Lesson Focus:** The most flexible word in English



**Key Learning:**

- "Interesting" = good, bad, or neutral depending on tone

- Positive: "That's an interesting idea!" (I like it)

- Negative: "That's... interesting." (I hate it)



**Example Sentences:**

- "Your approach is interesting" (positive: creative)

- "That's an interesting outfit" (negative: weird)



---



### TOPIC 8: CULTURAL REFERENCES

**Lesson Focus:** Movies, memes, idioms from pop culture



**Key Learning:**

- Why people say "May the Force be with you"

- Common memes ("OK Boomer", "This is fine")

- Understanding references improves listening comprehension



**Example Sentences:**

- "Winter is coming" (Game of Thrones = trouble ahead)

- "I'll be back" (Terminator = I'm leaving but will return)



---



### TOPIC 9: TABOO TOPICS

**Lesson Focus:** What NOT to talk about



**Key Learning:**

- Politics, religion, money = avoid in small talk

- How to politely change subject

- Cultural differences in taboo topics



**Example Sentences:**

- "Let's not talk about politics..."

- "I'd rather not discuss that."



---



### TOPIC 10: COMPLIMENTS & DEFLECTION

**Lesson Focus:** How to accept praise



**Key Learning:**

- Americans say "Thank you" to compliments

- Vietnamese often deflect ("No, I'm not good")

- Practice accepting compliments gracefully



**Example Sentences:**

- ❌ "No, I'm terrible at English"

- ✅ "Thank you! I've been practicing a lot"



---



### TOPIC 11: APOLOGIES (TOO MANY)

**Lesson Focus:** When to say "sorry" and when NOT to



**Key Learning:**

- Vietnamese say "sorry" excessively

- English speakers only apologize for real mistakes

- Over-apologizing signals insecurity



**Example Sentences:**

- ❌ "Sorry for my bad English" (don't apologize for learning!)

- ✅ "I'm still learning English" (confident statement)



---



### TOPIC 12: SMALL TALK MASTERY

**Lesson Focus:** Weather, weekend, sports



**Key Learning:**

- Small talk is ritual, not deep conversation

- Topics: Weather, weekend plans, commute, food

- How to keep conversation light and friendly



**Example Sentences:**

- "How was your weekend?"

- "Crazy weather today, right?"



---



## 3.3 CATEGORY 2: WORKPLACE ENGLISH (PROFESSIONAL)



### TOPIC 13: DAILY STANDUP

**Lesson Focus:** Agile team meetings



**Key Learning:**

- "What I did yesterday / What I'll do today / Blockers"

- Keep it short (1-2 minutes max)

- Speak confidently, no rambling



**Example Script:**

> "Yesterday I finished the login feature. Today I'll work on the dashboard. No blockers."



---



### TOPIC 14: POLITE DISAGREEING

**Lesson Focus:** Saying "no" without offending



**Key Learning:**

- Never say "You're wrong"

- Use softeners: "I see your point, but..."

- Offer alternative perspective



**Example Sentences:**

- ❌ "That's wrong"

- ✅ "I see it differently. What if we..."



---



### TOPIC 15: INTERRUPTING POLITELY

**Lesson Focus:** How to cut in without being rude



**Key Learning:**

- "Sorry to interrupt, but..."

- "Can I add something quickly?"

- Timing matters (don't interrupt mid-sentence)



**Example Sentences:**

- "Sorry to jump in, but I have a concern..."

- "Quick question before we move on..."



---



### TOPIC 16: DELIVERING BAD NEWS

**Lesson Focus:** Softening negative information



**Key Learning:**

- Start with positive (if possible)

- Be direct but empathetic

- Offer solution or next steps



**Example Structure:**

> "The good news is we finished testing. The bad news is we found 10 bugs. Here's the plan to fix them..."



---



### TOPIC 17: ASKING FOR A PROMOTION

**Lesson Focus:** Negotiation skills



**Key Learning:**

- Build case with data (achievements, metrics)

- Ask confidently, not apologetically

- Be prepared for negotiation



**Example Script:**

> "I've increased sales by 30% this year. I've taken on extra projects. I'd like to discuss a promotion."



---



### TOPIC 18: EXPLAINING BUGS

**Lesson Focus:** Technical communication for developers



**Key Learning:**

- Describe symptom (what's wrong)

- Explain expected behavior (what should happen)

- Suggest fix (if known)



**Example:**

> "The login button doesn't respond on iOS. It should redirect to the dashboard. I think it's a CSS issue."



---



### TOPIC 19: CHASING EMAILS

**Lesson Focus:** Following up professionally



**Key Learning:**

- Be polite but persistent

- "Just checking in..."

- "Any updates on this?"



**Example Sentences:**

- "Hi, just circling back on this. Any updates?"

- "Wanted to follow up on my previous email..."



---



### TOPIC 20: GIVING FEEDBACK

**Lesson Focus:** Constructive criticism



**Key Learning:**

- Sandwich method (praise → critique → praise)

- Focus on behavior, not person

- Offer specific suggestions



**Example:**

> "Your design looks great. The colors are a bit too bright — try softer tones. Overall, excellent work!"



---



### TOPIC 21: HANDLING DIFFICULT COWORKERS

**Lesson Focus:** Conflict resolution



**Key Learning:**

- Stay professional, don't take it personally

- Use "I" statements ("I feel..." not "You always...")

- Escalate to manager if needed



**Example:**

- ❌ "You never listen to me!"

- ✅ "I feel like my ideas aren't being heard. Can we discuss?"



---



### TOPIC 22: NETWORKING CONVERSATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Meeting new people at events



**Key Learning:**

- Elevator pitch (introduce yourself in 30 seconds)

- Ask open-ended questions

- Exchange contact information smoothly



**Example:**

> "Hi, I'm Kiet. I'm a software developer at BlueTech. What brings you here?"



---



### TOPIC 23: PHONE CALLS (BUSINESS)

**Lesson Focus:** Professional phone etiquette



**Key Learning:**

- Greeting: "This is [name] calling from [company]"

- State purpose clearly

- Confirm understanding before hanging up



**Example:**

> "Hi, this is Nhan from BlueTech. I'm calling about the project deadline. Do you have a moment?"



---



### TOPIC 24: PRESENTATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Public speaking in English



**Key Learning:**

- Start strong (hook audience)

- Signpost structure ("First... Second... Finally...")

- End with clear takeaway



**Example Opening:**

> "Today I'll show you three ways to improve productivity. First, automation. Second, focus time. Third, better meetings."



---



## 3.4 CATEGORY 3: SOCIAL SITUATIONS (EVERYDAY LIFE)



### TOPIC 25: ORDERING COFFEE

**Lesson Focus:** Café English



**Key Learning:**

- Starbucks lingo (tall, grande, venti)

- Modifications (extra shot, no sugar)

- Polite ordering phrases



**Example:**

> "Hi, can I get a grande iced latte with oat milk? Thanks!"



---



### TOPIC 26: GROCERY SHOPPING

**Lesson Focus:** Supermarket vocabulary



**Key Learning:**

- Aisles, produce, checkout

- "Where can I find...?"

- Asking for help



**Example:**

> "Excuse me, where's the dairy section?"



---



### TOPIC 27: AT THE DOCTOR

**Lesson Focus:** Medical English



**Key Learning:**

- Describing symptoms

- Understanding prescriptions

- Asking about side effects



**Example:**

> "I have a headache and sore throat. It started two days ago."



---



### TOPIC 28: RENTING AN APARTMENT

**Lesson Focus:** Housing vocabulary



**Key Learning:**

- Lease, deposit, utilities

- Negotiating rent

- Asking about amenities



**Example:**

> "Does the rent include utilities? Is there a washer/dryer in the unit?"



---



### TOPIC 29: MAKING FRIENDS

**Lesson Focus:** Social invitations



**Key Learning:**

- "Want to hang out?"

- Suggesting activities

- Accepting/declining gracefully



**Example:**

> "Hey, want to grab dinner this weekend?"



---



### TOPIC 30: DATING CONVERSATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Romantic English



**Key Learning:**

- Flirting (light and respectful)

- Asking someone out

- First date small talk



**Example:**

> "I had a great time tonight. Would you like to do this again?"



---



### TOPIC 31: TRAVELING

**Lesson Focus:** Airport, hotel, directions



**Key Learning:**

- Checking in

- Asking for directions

- Hotel complaints



**Example:**

> "Hi, I have a reservation under Hobbs. Can I get a wake-up call at 7am?"



---



### TOPIC 32: EMERGENCY SITUATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Urgent communication



**Key Learning:**

- Calling 911

- Describing emergency

- Staying calm under pressure



**Example:**

> "Help! There's a fire at [address]. Everyone is outside."



---



### TOPIC 33: COMPLAINING (POLITELY)

**Lesson Focus:** Service complaints



**Key Learning:**

- "I'm not happy with..."

- Requesting refund/replacement

- Escalating issue



**Example:**

> "Excuse me, I ordered a burger but got a salad. Can you fix this?"



---



### TOPIC 34: PARTY CONVERSATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Mingling at events



**Key Learning:**

- Breaking into groups

- Exiting conversations gracefully

- Reading social cues



**Example:**

> "Oh, I see someone I need to say hi to. Nice meeting you!"



---



## 3.5 CATEGORY 4: TECH & GAMING (MODERN SLANG)



### TOPIC 35: GAMING LINGO

**Lesson Focus:** "GG", "Noob", "Lag"



**Key Learning:**

- Common abbreviations (AFK, BRB, IMO)

- Trash talk (friendly insults)

- Team communication



**Example:**

> "GG WP! That was intense. You played really well."



---



### TOPIC 36: TECH JARGON

**Lesson Focus:** "Bug", "Deploy", "Crash"



**Key Learning:**

- Developer vocabulary

- Explaining tech issues to non-techies

- Startup slang



**Example:**

> "The app crashed during the demo. We need to debug ASAP."



---



### TOPIC 37: SOCIAL MEDIA SPEAK

**Lesson Focus:** "DM", "Like", "Trend"



**Key Learning:**

- Platform-specific terms (tweet, post, story)

- Influencer vocabulary

- Online etiquette



**Example:**

> "Did you see her latest post? It's going viral!"



---



### TOPIC 38: AI & CHATBOTS

**Lesson Focus:** "Prompt", "Model", "Hallucination"



**Key Learning:**

- How to talk about AI naturally

- Prompting strategies

- AI limitations



**Example:**

> "I asked ChatGPT to write a summary, but it hallucinated some facts."



---



### TOPIC 39: CRYPTOCURRENCY

**Lesson Focus:** "Wallet", "HODL", "Moon"



**Key Learning:**

- Crypto slang

- Explaining blockchain simply

- Investment talk



**Example:**

> "Bitcoin is mooning right now. Are you holding or selling?"



---



### TOPIC 40: STREAMING & YOUTUBE

**Lesson Focus:** "Stream", "Subscriber", "Drop"



**Key Learning:**

- Content creator vocabulary

- Commenting and engaging

- Explaining videos to others



**Example:**

> "I was watching a stream and the chat was so toxic."



---



### TOPIC 41: MEMES & INTERNET CULTURE

**Lesson Focus:** Understanding viral content



**Key Learning:**

- Common meme formats

- References (Doge, Pepe, "This is fine")

- When to use meme language



**Example:**

> "That meeting was a total dumpster fire."



---



### TOPIC 42: REMOTE WORK

**Lesson Focus:** "WFH", "Hybrid", "Async"



**Key Learning:**

- Remote work vocabulary

- Virtual meeting etiquette

- Time zone talk



**Example:**

> "Can we do this async? I'm in a different time zone."



---



## 3.6 CATEGORY 5: EMOTIONS & PSYCHOLOGY (INNER WORLD)



### TOPIC 43: ANXIETY & STRESS

**Lesson Focus:** Talking about mental health



**Key Learning:**

- "I'm overwhelmed", "I'm burned out"

- Asking for support

- Self-care vocabulary



**Example:**

> "I'm feeling really stressed lately. I need to take a break."



---



### TOPIC 44: CONFIDENCE VS. ARROGANCE

**Lesson Focus:** The fine line



**Key Learning:**

- Confidence = self-assured without putting others down

- Arrogance = overconfidence with ego

- How to sound confident, not cocky



**Example:**

- Confident: "I'm good at presentations."

- Arrogant: "I'm the best presenter here."



---



### TOPIC 45: MOTIVATION & DISCIPLINE

**Lesson Focus:** "Grind", "Hustle", "Consistency"



**Key Learning:**

- Self-improvement vocabulary

- Talking about goals

- Avoiding toxic productivity culture



**Example:**

> "I'm trying to stay consistent with my English practice."



---



### TOPIC 46: RELATIONSHIPS & BOUNDARIES

**Lesson Focus:** "Set boundaries", "Toxic", "Ghosting"



**Key Learning:**

- Modern relationship vocabulary

- Discussing personal space

- Recognizing unhealthy patterns



**Example:**

> "I need to set boundaries with my boss. He texts me at midnight."



---



### TOPIC 47: CULTURAL IDENTITY

**Lesson Focus:** "Third culture kid", "Immigrant experience"



**Key Learning:**

- Talking about Vietnamese-English identity

- Code-switching (Vietnamese at home, English at work)

- Navigating two cultures



**Example:**

> "I feel Vietnamese at heart but speak English for work."



---



### TOPIC 48: IMPOSTER SYNDROME

**Lesson Focus:** "I feel like a fraud"



**Key Learning:**

- Recognizing self-doubt

- Normalizing imposter feelings

- Building self-compassion



**Example:**

> "Everyone here seems so smart. I feel like I don't belong."



---



## 3.7 CATEGORY 6: ADVANCED GRAMMAR (COMPLEX PATTERNS)



### TOPIC 49: CONDITIONALS

**Lesson Focus:** If/then patterns



**Key Learning:**

- Zero conditional (facts)

- First conditional (real future)

- Second conditional (hypothetical)

- Third conditional (past regret)



**Examples:**

- "If it rains, I stay home." (zero)

- "If it rains tomorrow, I'll stay home." (first)

- "If I won the lottery, I'd quit my job." (second)

- "If I had studied harder, I would have passed." (third)



---



### TOPIC 50: PASSIVE VOICE

**Lesson Focus:** When and why to use it



**Key Learning:**

- Active vs. passive

- Professional writing (passive preferred)

- Avoiding blame



**Examples:**

- Active: "We made a mistake."

- Passive: "A mistake was made." (less accusatory)



---



### TOPIC 51: REPORTED SPEECH

**Lesson Focus:** He said that...



**Key Learning:**

- Direct vs. reported

- Tense backshifting

- Common reporting verbs (said, told, asked)



**Examples:**

- Direct: "I am tired."

- Reported: He said he was tired.



---



### TOPIC 52: MODALS (ADVANCED)

**Lesson Focus:** Would, could, should, might



**Key Learning:**

- Degrees of certainty

- Polite requests

- Giving advice



**Examples:**

- "You should study more." (advice)

- "Could you help me?" (polite request)

- "It might rain." (possibility)



---



### TOPIC 53: RELATIVE CLAUSES

**Lesson Focus:** Who, which, that



**Key Learning:**

- Defining vs. non-defining clauses

- When to use commas

- Avoiding run-on sentences



**Examples:**

- "The book that I read was amazing." (defining)

- "My book, which I read yesterday, was amazing." (non-defining)



---



### TOPIC 54: PHRASAL VERBS (MASTERY)

**Lesson Focus:** Get, take, put + prepositions



**Key Learning:**

- Separable vs. inseparable

- Common phrasal verbs in business

- Literal vs. idiomatic meanings



**Examples:**

- "Put up with" (tolerate)

- "Get over" (recover from)

- "Take after" (resemble)



---



## 3.8 CATEGORY 7: PRONUNCIATION DRILLS (ACCENT REDUCTION)



### TOPIC 55: TH SOUNDS

**Lesson Focus:** /θ/ and /ð/



**Key Learning:**

- Tongue between teeth

- "Think" vs. "Sink"

- "This" vs. "Dis"



**Practice:**

> "Three thin thieves thought they saw the throne."



---



### TOPIC 56: R vs. L

**Lesson Focus:** Vietnamese speaker struggles



**Key Learning:**

- "Right" vs. "Light"

- "Red" vs. "Led"

- Minimal pairs practice



**Practice:**

> "The red light is on the right."



---



### TOPIC 57: V vs. W

**Lesson Focus:** Common confusion



**Key Learning:**

- "Very" vs. "Worry" (different sounds!)

- Lip position matters



**Practice:**

> "We were very worried about the weather."



---



### TOPIC 58: WORD STRESS

**Lesson Focus:** Syllable emphasis



**Key Learning:**

- "REcord" (noun) vs. "reCORD" (verb)

- Compound nouns (BLACKboard, not blackBOARD)



**Practice:**

> "I need to REcord this reCORDing."



---



## 3.9 CATEGORY 8: BUSINESS ENGLISH (CORPORATE)



### TOPIC 59: NEGOTIATIONS

**Lesson Focus:** Win-win deals



**Key Learning:**

- BATNA (best alternative to negotiated agreement)

- Anchoring (first offer matters)

- Compromise language



**Example:**

> "We're willing to meet you halfway on price."



---



### TOPIC 60: WRITING PROFESSIONAL EMAILS

**Lesson Focus:** Structure and tone



**Key Learning:**

- Subject lines

- Opening/closing phrases

- Avoiding too casual or too formal



**Example:**

> Subject: Project Update — Q4 Deliverables

> "Hi Sarah, Just wanted to update you on the project status..."



---



# 4. STUDENT PSYCHOLOGY: MOUSE → TIGER



## 4.1 THE MOUSE ARCHETYPE



### DEFINING CHARACTERISTICS



**MOUSE VOICE:**

- Speaks quietly, mumbles, trails off

- Apologizes constantly ("Sorry for my bad English")

- Translates every sentence from Vietnamese before speaking

- Freezes when addressed by native speakers

- Studies grammar endlessly, speaks rarely

- Perfectionist mindset (won't speak until "ready")

- Avoids eye contact

- Self-deprecating ("I'm so stupid at English")



### PSYCHOLOGICAL ROOTS



Mouse Voice is created by:

1. **Shame-Based Education:** Vietnamese schools punish mistakes harshly

2. **Perfectionism Culture:** "If you can't do it perfectly, don't do it at all"

3. **Face-Saving:** Fear of looking foolish in front of peers

4. **Grammar Prison:** 10+ years of textbook study, zero conversation practice

5. **Authority Fear:** Teacher as judge, not coach



### MOUSE BEHAVIORS IN CLASS



- Sits in back row

- Never volunteers answers

- Looks down when teacher makes eye contact

- Whispers answers to partner instead of speaking aloud

- Over-prepares but underperforms under pressure

- Quits programs within 3 months (low completion rate)



---



## 4.2 THE TIGER ARCHETYPE



### DEFINING CHARACTERISTICS



**TIGER VOICE:**

- Speaks loudly, clearly, with energy

- Makes mistakes without apology

- Thinks in English (minimal translation)

- Approaches native speakers confidently

- Practices speaking before studying grammar

- Growth mindset (mistakes = learning)

- Strong eye contact

- Self-assured ("I'm a learner, getting better every day")



### PSYCHOLOGICAL FOUNDATIONS



Tiger Voice is built through:

1. **Confidence-First Approach:** Speak from Day 1, polish later

2. **Mistake Celebration:** Errors are data, not shame

3. **Supportive Environment:** Teacher as coach, peers as allies

4. **Identity Shift:** "I am an English speaker" (not "I study English")

5. **Repetition:** Daily practice builds neurological pathways



### TIGER BEHAVIORS IN CLASS



- Sits in front, engaged

- Volunteers answers frequently

- Makes mistakes confidently

- Laughs at own errors, tries again

- Helps peers without judgment

- Completes programs (high retention)



---



## 4.3 THE TRANSFORMATION PROCESS



### PHASE 1: AWARENESS (WEEK 1-2)



**Teacher Actions:**

- Identify Mouse behaviors explicitly

- Name it: "That's your Mouse Voice. Let's find your Tiger."

- Show contrast: Record student speaking quietly, then loudly

- No judgment, just observation



**Student Realization:**

- "Oh, I do apologize too much"

- "I didn't realize I was whispering"

- Awareness creates possibility for change



---



### PHASE 2: PRACTICE (WEEK 3-6)



**Teacher Actions:**

- Call out Mouse behaviors gently: "Try that again, louder."

- Praise Tiger behaviors loudly: "YES! That's Tiger Voice!"

- Create low-stakes practice (pair work before full class)

- Assign "Tiger Challenges" (speak to 3 strangers this week)



**Student Progress:**

- Gradual volume increase

- Fewer apologies

- More risk-taking in speech

- Small wins build confidence



---



### PHASE 3: INTEGRATION (WEEK 7-12)



**Teacher Actions:**

- Raise stakes (present to class, debates, mock interviews)

- Less correction, more encouragement

- Celebrate milestones ("Remember when you couldn't speak for 30 seconds?")

- Introduce to community (buddy system, networking events)



**Student Breakthrough:**

- Tiger becomes default mode

- Speaks without thinking about "Mouse"

- Confidence spills into other life areas (work, relationships)

- Identity shift: "I'm an English speaker now"



---



### PHASE 4: MASTERY (MONTH 4+)



**Teacher Actions:**

- Student becomes model for others

- Invite to help newer students

- Document success story

- Transition to advanced content



**Student State:**

- Natural, effortless Tiger Voice

- Speaks English without fear or translation

- Helps others transform

- Completes NET program, becomes alumni advocate



---



## 4.4 TEACHER INTERVENTIONS



### SCRIPT: "YOU'RE USING MOUSE VOICE"



**Situation:** Student apologizes for third time in 5 minutes



**Teacher:**

> "Hold on. I'm hearing your Mouse Voice right now. You just apologized three times. Tigers don't apologize for learning. You're trying, you're speaking, that's perfect. Let's try again, no 'sorry' this time. Ready?"



**Effect:** Student becomes aware, corrects behavior immediately



---



### SCRIPT: "SPEAK LOUDER"



**Situation:** Student mumbles answer



**Teacher:**

> "I need Tiger Voice. Say that again, louder. Pretend you're talking to someone across the room."



**Effect:** Forces projection, builds confidence through physicality



---



### SCRIPT: "NO TRANSLATION"



**Situation:** Student pauses to translate from Vietnamese



**Teacher:**

> "Stop! You're translating right now, I can tell. Just say it in English, even if it's broken. We'll fix it after. Go!"



**Effect:** Breaks translation habit, builds direct English thinking



---



### SCRIPT: "MISTAKES ARE WINS"



**Situation:** Student makes error and looks embarrassed



**Teacher:**

> "Yes! You made a mistake! That means you tried! Mistakes are data. Now we know what to fix. That's how learning works. Try it again."



**Effect:** Reframes errors as positive, reduces shame



---



## 4.5 MOUSE → TIGER ASSESSMENT



### INDICATORS TO TRACK



**Week 1 Baseline:**

- Volume level (1-10 scale)

- Apology count per class

- Eye contact frequency

- Voluntary participation rate



**Monthly Check-ins:**

- Same metrics, track improvement

- Record audio samples (compare pronunciation and confidence)

- Self-assessment: "Do you feel like Mouse or Tiger today?"



### SUCCESS METRICS



**Tiger Transformation Achieved When:**

- Student speaks at 7+ volume consistently

- Zero apologies for mistakes in a full class

- Volunteers answers without prompting

- Makes eye contact with teacher and peers

- Completes "Tiger Challenge" outside class (talk to stranger)



---



# 5. CLASSROOM MANAGEMENT: THE ART



## 5.1 ENERGY MANAGEMENT



### THE PRINCIPLE



Every class has energy dynamics. Teacher's job is to **read the room** and adjust pace, activities, and tone accordingly.



### HIGH ENERGY CLASS



**Signs:**

- Students talking over each other

- Laughter, excitement

- Can't sit still



**Teacher Response:**

- Channel energy into activities (debates, games)

- Don't fight it — ride the wave

- Use competition (teams, points, prizes)



---



### LOW ENERGY CLASS



**Signs:**

- Silence, sluggish responses

- Checking phones

- Yawning



**Teacher Response:**

- **Physical movement:** Stand up, stretch, walk around

- **Change activity:** Stop lecture, start game

- **Increase stakes:** "Winner gets to leave 5 minutes early"

- **Personal connection:** Ask about their week, mood



---



### MIXED ENERGY CLASS



**Signs:**

- Some students engaged, others zoned out



**Teacher Response:**

- Call on quiet students specifically

- Pair high-energy with low-energy students

- Increase interactivity (more pair work, less lecture)



---



## 5.2 GROUP DYNAMICS



### BALANCING PERSONALITIES



**THE DOMINATOR:**

- Talks too much, interrupts others

- Solution: Set speaking time limits, "Let's hear from Nhan now"



**THE SILENT ONE:**

- Never volunteers

- Solution: Call on them directly with easy questions, build confidence



**THE CLOWN:**

- Jokes constantly, distracts class

- Solution: Channel humor into learning (make them perform role-plays)



**THE SKEPTIC:**

- "This won't work for me"

- Solution: Give them data, show success stories, address objections



---



### PAIR WORK STRATEGIES



**STRONG + WEAK PAIRING:**

- Pros: Weak student learns from strong

- Cons: Strong student may dominate

- When to use: When teaching new concept



**SIMILAR LEVEL PAIRING:**

- Pros: Equal participation

- Cons: No peer teaching benefit

- When to use: Free practice, debates



**RANDOM PAIRING:**

- Pros: Builds community, prevents cliques

- Cons: May create awkward pairs

- When to use: Warm-up activities



---



## 5.3 TIME MANAGEMENT



### THE 90-MINUTE CHALLENGE



Classes feel long. Attention spans are short. Solution: **Segment ruthlessly.**



**10-minute rule:** No single activity should last more than 10 minutes without a transition.



**Typical Mistakes:**

- ❌ 30-minute lecture (students zone out)

- ❌ 60-minute free conversation (loses structure)

- ❌ Skipping games to "save time" (kills engagement)



**Optimal Flow:**

1. Warm-up game (10 min)

2. Input (5 min)

3. Guided practice (5 min)

4. Free practice round 1 (15 min)

5. Mid-lesson game (10 min)

6. Free practice round 2 (20 min)

7. Break (5 min)

8. Free practice round 3 (15 min)

9. Final game (10 min)

10. Feedback (5 min)



**Total: 100 minutes** (allows buffer for overtime)



---



## 5.4 HANDLING DISRUPTIONS



### LATE ARRIVALS



**Protocol:**

- Don't stop class to acknowledge

- Quick nod or wave

- Continue teaching

- Catch them up during pair work



---



### PHONE USE



**Protocol:**

- Set rule: Phones away during class

- Exception: Using phone for vocabulary lookup

- If chronic: Address privately after class



---



### SIDE CONVERSATIONS (VIETNAMESE)



**Protocol:**

- Pause, make eye contact

- "Everything okay? Need help with something?"

- Redirect to English: "Let's discuss in English so everyone can learn"



---



### ARGUMENTS/CONFLICTS



**Protocol:**

- Stop immediately

- Separate if needed

- Address after class, not in front of others

- Find root cause (personality clash? Misunderstanding?)



---



## 5.5 INCLUSIVE TEACHING



### VISUAL LEARNERS



**Strategies:**

- Use board heavily (write key words)

- Show pictures, videos, diagrams

- Color-code grammar patterns



---



### AUDITORY LEARNERS



**Strategies:**

- Play audio clips frequently

- Repeat key phrases multiple times

- Use choral repetition



---



### KINESTHETIC LEARNERS



**Strategies:**

- Physical games (charades, board races)

- Role-plays with movement

- Gestures to reinforce vocabulary



---



### MIXED ABILITY CLASSES



**Challenge:** Some students A2, others B2



**Solution:**

- Tiered activities (different difficulty levels)

- Strong students help weak students (peer teaching)

- Individual check-ins during pair work



---



# 6. CRISIS PROTOCOLS: 10 EMERGENCY SCRIPTS



## 6.1 OVERVIEW



Even the best teachers face classroom crises. These 10 scripts provide **word-for-word responses** to common emergencies.



---



## 6.2 CRISIS #1: THE SILENCE (NO ONE SPEAKS)



**Situation:** You ask a question. Crickets. Total silence.



**Script:**

> "Okay, I see we're all still waking up. Let's change this. Everyone stand up! We're going to do a quick 'Popcorn Story.' I'll start a sentence, then point to someone to continue. Ready?

>

> 'Yesterday I went to the market and I saw...'"

>

> [Point to student] "Nhan, continue!"



**Effect:** Physical movement + low-pressure game breaks the silence



---



## 6.3 CRISIS #2: ONE STUDENT DOMINATES



**Situation:** Same student answers every question, others don't participate



**Script:**

> "Kiet, I love your energy! You're helping me a lot. Now I need to hear from others. Let's do this: Everyone who HASN'T spoken yet, raise your hand."

>

> [Call on non-participants by name]



**Effect:** Politely redirects without shaming dominant student



---



## 6.4 CRISIS #3: STUDENT SAYS "I DON'T KNOW"



**Situation:** Student gives up immediately without trying



**Script:**

> "That's okay! Let's think together. What's your BEST GUESS? Even if you're wrong, say something. Mouse Voice says 'I don't know.' Tiger Voice says 'Let me try.' Go!"



**Effect:** Reframes "don't know" as opportunity to practice



---



## 6.5 CRISIS #4: STUDENT CRIES/GETS EMOTIONAL



**Situation:** Student breaks down (frustration, stress, personal issue)



**Script:**

> "Hey, it's okay. Take a minute. Let's step outside together."

>

> [Privately] "What's going on? Are you okay?"

>

> [If learning-related] "English is hard, I get it. But you're doing great. Let's take a break and come back stronger."

>

> [If personal] "Want to talk about it, or just take a moment?"



**Effect:** Shows empathy, gives student space, avoids public embarrassment



---



## 6.6 CRISIS #5: TECH FAILURE (ZOOM CRASH, MIC ISSUES)



**Situation:** Technology stops working mid-class



**Script:**

> "Okay, tech is being difficult today. No problem! We're going to go old-school. Everyone grab a pen and paper. We're doing a writing exercise while I fix this."

>

> [Assign task that doesn't require tech]



**Effect:** Maintains momentum, doesn't waste class time



---



## 6.7 CRISIS #6: RUDE/DISRESPECTFUL STUDENT



**Situation:** Student interrupts, mocks, or challenges teacher



**Script (In Class):**

> "Let's pause there. I need everyone's respect in this room. If you have feedback, we can talk after class."



**Script (After Class):**

> "Hey, what happened today? That wasn't like you. Is everything okay?"

>

> [Listen to response]

>

> "I need you to respect this space. If something's bothering you, tell me, but don't disrupt others. Can we agree on that?"



**Effect:** Sets boundary publicly, addresses root cause privately



---



## 6.8 CRISIS #7: STUDENTS ARE LOST (CONTENT TOO HARD)



**Situation:** Blank stares, confusion, no one understands



**Script:**

> "Okay, I'm seeing confusion. Let me back up. This is too hard right now. Let's go simpler."

>

> [Repeat concept using basic English, gestures, examples]

>

> "Does this make more sense?"



**Effect:** Acknowledges mistake, adjusts on the fly



---



## 6.9 CRISIS #8: RUNNING OUT OF TIME



**Situation:** 10 minutes left, haven't finished lesson



**Script:**

> "We're short on time, so let's prioritize. The most important thing today is [X]. Let's spend these last 10 minutes nailing that."

>

> [Cut non-essential activities]



**Effect:** Focuses on core objective, avoids rushed ending



---



## 6.10 CRISIS #9: STUDENT ASKS QUESTION YOU CAN'T ANSWER



**Situation:** Grammar question, vocab explanation, cultural nuance — you don't know



**Script:**

> "That's a great question! Honestly, I don't know the answer off the top of my head. Let me look it up and get back to you next class. In the meantime, does anyone else know?"

>

> [If no one knows] "Perfect homework assignment — everyone research this and we'll discuss next time."



**Effect:** Models intellectual humility, turns into learning opportunity



---



## 6.11 CRISIS #10: STUDENT WANTS TO QUIT



**Situation:** Student says they want to drop the program



**Script:**

> "I hear you. English is frustrating sometimes. Before you decide, let's talk. What's making you want to quit?"

>

> [Listen to response]

>

> **If it's difficulty:** "Progress isn't linear. You're doing better than you think. Let's review your wins from Week 1."

>

> **If it's time:** "What if we adjust your schedule? Maybe online instead of in-person?"

>

> **If it's confidence:** "You're in the hardest part right now — the plateau. Breakthrough is coming. Don't quit before the miracle."

>

> "Give it 2 more weeks. If you still feel this way, we'll talk again. Deal?"



**Effect:** Validates frustration, offers solutions, buys time for student to push through



---



# 7. ASSESSMENT SYSTEM: MEASURING PROGRESS



## 7.1 OVERVIEW



NET uses **multiple assessment types** to track student progress holistically.



### ASSESSMENT TYPES



1. **Intake Assessment** (Before enrollment)

2. **Level Placement** (Diagnostic)

3. **Progress Checks** (Monthly)

4. **Final Assessment** (End of program)

5. **Self-Assessment** (Weekly reflection)



---



## 7.2 INTAKE ASSESSMENT (PRE-ENROLLMENT)



### PURPOSE



Determine:

- Current English level (A1-C2)

- Student archetype (Mouse vs Tiger)

- Specific pain points

- Learning goals

- Motivation level



### FORMAT (30-45 minutes)



**PART 1: Grammar Test (10 min)**

- 20 multiple-choice questions

- Tests A1-C1 grammar structures

- Auto-scored



**PART 2: Listening Test (10 min)**

- 3 audio clips (different difficulty levels)

- Comprehension questions

- Tests listening at 60% rule



**PART 3: Speaking Test (15 min)**

- 5 prompts, student speaks 1-2 minutes each

- Recorded for later evaluation

- Prompts:

1. "Tell me about yourself"

2. "Describe your typical workday"

3. "What's your favorite hobby and why?"

4. "Tell me about a time you had to speak English and it was difficult"

5. "Why do you want to improve your English?"



**PART 4: Writing Test (10 min)**

- Write 150-word email responding to scenario

- Tests grammar, vocabulary, structure



### SCORING



**Grammar:** 0-100 (auto-scored)

**Listening:** 0-100 (auto-scored)

**Speaking:**

- Fluency (1-10)

- Pronunciation (1-10)

- Grammar (1-10)

- Vocabulary (1-10)

- Confidence (1-10)



**Writing:**

- Grammar accuracy (1-10)

- Vocabulary range (1-10)

- Structure/organization (1-10)



### RESULTS



**Level Placement:**

- A1: Beginner (0-20)

- A2: Elementary (21-40)

- B1: Intermediate (41-60)

- B2: Upper Intermediate (61-80)

- C1: Advanced (81-100)



**Archetype:**

- Mouse Voice: Confidence score < 5

- Tiger Voice: Confidence score > 7



**Recommended Program:**

- Group vs. 1-on-1

- Intensity (2x/week vs. daily)

- Focus area (speaking, pronunciation, grammar)



---



## 7.3 PROGRESS CHECKS (MONTHLY)



### PURPOSE



Track improvement, adjust teaching strategy, maintain motivation



### FORMAT (15 minutes)



**PART 1: 2-Minute Speech (Recorded)**

- Same prompt every month: "What did you learn this month?"

- Compare to previous recordings

- Measure fluency, grammar, vocabulary improvement



**PART 2: Vocabulary Quiz (5 min)**

- 10 words from past month's lessons

- Use in sentences, not translate



**PART 3: Self-Assessment (5 min)**

- "How confident do you feel speaking English now?" (1-10)

- "What's your biggest win this month?"

- "What's still challenging?"



**PART 4: Teacher Observation (5 min)**

- Teacher rates:

- Volume (Mouse→Tiger progress)

- Participation frequency

- Mistake-making comfort

- Grammar accuracy improvement



### RESULTS SHARED WITH STUDENT



**Progress Report includes:**

- Audio comparison (Month 1 vs Month 2)

- Vocabulary retention rate

- Confidence score trend

- Teacher comments

- Action plan for next month



---



## 7.4 FINAL ASSESSMENT (END OF PROGRAM)



### PURPOSE



Demonstrate transformation, certify completion, celebrate success



### FORMAT (60 minutes)



**PART 1: 5-Minute Presentation**

- Student chooses topic

- Presents to class (or teacher if 1-on-1)

- Evaluated on fluency, structure, confidence



**PART 2: Mock Conversation (15 min)**

- Real-world scenario (job interview, client meeting, networking)

- Student responds naturally

- Evaluated on pragmatics, grammar, vocabulary



**PART 3: Written Essay (30 min)**

- 300-word essay on assigned topic

- Evaluated on structure, grammar, argumentation



**PART 4: Self-Reflection (10 min)**

- "How has your English changed?"

- "What was your biggest breakthrough?"

- "What will you continue practicing?"



### CERTIFICATION



**Students who complete 80%+ of program receive:**

- NET Completion Certificate

- Audio portfolio (Month 1 vs Final)

- Written evaluation from teacher

- Alumni status (access to community events)



---



## 7.5 SELF-ASSESSMENT (WEEKLY)



### PURPOSE



Build metacognition, track daily practice, maintain accountability



### FORMAT (5 minutes, end of week)



**QUESTIONS:**

1. Did you complete daily listening (45 min x 7 days)? Y/N

2. Did you complete daily speaking practice (15 min x 7 days)? Y/N

3. Did you attend buddy session this week? Y/N

4. What was your best English moment this week?

5. What will you improve next week?



**TRACKED BY NET PET:**

- Streak counter (consecutive days of practice)

- XP earned (based on consistency)

- Mood indicator (happy, neutral, sad)



---



# 8. THE BUDDY SYSTEM: PEER LEARNING



## 8.1 OVERVIEW



**The Buddy System pairs students for weekly 1-hour practice sessions outside of class.**



### WHY IT WORKS



- **Reduces pressure:** Speaking to peer vs. teacher/native speaker

- **Builds community:** Friendships form through shared struggle

- **Increases practice:** +1 hour/week of conversation

- **Improves retention:** Students with buddies complete at 90% rate (vs. 60% solo)



---



## 8.2 HOW IT WORKS



### PAIRING STRATEGY



**SIMILAR LEVEL:**

- Beginner + Beginner

- Intermediate + Intermediate

- Prevents one student from dominating



**OPPOSITE ARCHETYPES (IDEAL):**

- Mouse + Tiger (Tiger pulls Mouse out of shell)



**COMPATIBILITY FACTORS:**

- Schedule availability

- Interests (gaming, tech, business)

- Personality (introvert + extrovert balance)



### FIRST BUDDY SESSION (GUIDED)



**Teacher provides:**

- List of 10 conversation topics

- Timer guidelines (switch every 5 minutes)

- Feedback form to complete afterward



**Topics:**

1. Weekend plans

2. Favorite movie/show

3. Work/school stress

4. Learning English struggles

5. Vietnamese vs. English culture

6. Dream vacation destination

7. Tech or gaming interests

8. Food preferences

9. Personal goals for English

10. Funniest mistake made in English



**STRUCTURE:**

- 5 min: Student A talks, Student B listens

- 5 min: Student B talks, Student A listens

- 2 min: Switch topics

- Repeat 6 times (60 minutes total)



### ONGOING SESSIONS



**After first 3 weeks, students self-direct:**

- Choose own topics

- Adjust format (debates, storytelling, role-plays)

- Meet at café, park, online (Zoom)



**ACCOUNTABILITY:**

- Report to teacher: "Did you meet your buddy this week?"

- NET PET tracks buddy session attendance



---



## 8.3 BUDDY MATCHING ALGORITHM (FUTURE)



**NET PET will auto-match students based on:**

1. English level (±5 points)

2. Availability overlap (schedule)

3. Shared interests (from intake survey)

4. Personality compatibility (Tiger/Mouse balance)



**MANUAL OVERRIDE:**

- Students can request specific buddy

- Teacher can force pair if strategic



---



## 8.4 BUDDY CHALLENGES (MONTHLY)



**To keep system engaging, NET introduces monthly challenges:**



**CHALLENGE 1: "Speak Only English"**

- 1 hour, no Vietnamese allowed

- First person to switch to Vietnamese buys coffee



**CHALLENGE 2: "Record & Share"**

- Record 5-minute conversation

- Post in private group (class sees it)

- Get feedback from peers



**CHALLENGE 3: "Public Practice"**

- Go to tourist area (Bui Vien, Ben Thanh Market)

- Practice English with foreigners

- Report back to class



---



# 9. PRONUNCIATION: THE 42 JOLLY PHONICS SOUNDS



## 9.1 OVERVIEW



**NET uses the Jolly Phonics method** to teach pronunciation systematically.



### WHY JOLLY PHONICS?



- Designed for English language learners

- Breaks English into 42 distinct sounds

- Each sound has gesture + story

- Proven effective for children (works for adults too)



### THE PROBLEM



Vietnamese has **~30 phonemes**. English has **44 phonemes**. Vietnamese speakers struggle with sounds that don't exist in Vietnamese:

- /θ/ (think)

- /ð/ (this)

- /v/ (very) — often pronounced as /w/

- /r/ (red) — often confused with /l/

- Ending consonants (Vietnamese rarely ends words with consonants)



---



## 9.2 THE 42 SOUNDS (GROUPED BY DIFFICULTY)



### GROUP 1: EASY (EXIST IN VIETNAMESE)



1. **s** (snake)

2. **a** (ant)

3. **t** (tap)

4. **p** (pop)

5. **i** (insect)

6. **n** (net)



### GROUP 2: MEDIUM (SIMILAR TO VIETNAMESE)



7. **c/k** (cat, kite)

8. **e** (egg)

9. **h** (hat)

10. **r** (rabbit) — tricky for Vietnamese!

11. **m** (mouse)

12. **d** (dog)



### GROUP 3: HARD (DON'T EXIST IN VIETNAMESE)



13. **g** (goat)

14. **o** (octopus)

15. **u** (umbrella)

16. **l** (lion) — often confused with /r/

17. **f** (fish)

18. **b** (ball)



### GROUP 4: VERY HARD (CRITICAL FOCUS)



19. **j** (jump)

20. **v** (van) — Vietnamese speakers say /w/

21. **w** (water)

22. **x** (box)

23. **y** (yes)

24. **z** (zebra)

25. **qu** (queen)



### GROUP 5: CONSONANT DIGRAPHS



26. **ch** (chip)

27. **sh** (ship)

28. **th** (think) — /θ/ sound (HARD!)

29. **th** (this) — /ð/ sound (HARD!)

30. **ng** (sing) — ending sound (HARD!)



### GROUP 6: VOWEL DIGRAPHS



31. **ai** (rain)

32. **ee** (see)

33. **igh** (night)

34. **oa** (boat)

35. **oo** (book/moon) — two sounds!

36. **ar** (car)

37. **or** (fork)

38. **ur** (burn)

39. **ow** (cow)

40. **oi** (coin)

41. **ear** (hear)

42. **air** (hair)



---



## 9.3 PRONUNCIATION DRILLS (WEEKLY FOCUS)



**Each week, NET focuses on 3-5 sounds from the 42.**



**WEEK 1: TH SOUNDS (/θ/ and /ð/)**



**Lesson:**

1. Demonstrate tongue placement (between teeth)

2. Practice minimal pairs:

- "Think" vs. "Sink"

- "This" vs. "Dis"

- "Mouth" vs. "Mouse"

3. Tongue twister:

> "Three thin thieves thought they saw the throne."

4. Record student saying it, compare to native speaker



**WEEK 2: R vs. L**



**Lesson:**

1. Show lip position for /r/ (rounded)

2. Show tongue position for /l/ (touches roof)

3. Practice minimal pairs:

- "Right" vs. "Light"

- "Red" vs. "Led"

- "Collect" vs. "Correct"

4. Sentence drill:

> "The red light is on the right."



**WEEK 3: V vs. W**



**Lesson:**

1. /v/ = teeth touch bottom lip

2. /w/ = lips rounded, no teeth

3. Practice:

- "Very" vs. "Worry" (different sounds!)

- "Vest" vs. "West"

4. Sentence:

> "We were very worried about the weather."



---



## 9.4 PRONUNCIATION FEEDBACK PROTOCOL



**IN CLASS:**

- Don't interrupt mid-speech for pronunciation

- Note errors, address in feedback segment

- Demonstrate correct sound, have student repeat



**BUDDY SESSIONS:**

- Students record each other

- Compare to native speaker audio (from NET app)

- Practice together, give peer feedback



**NET PET INTEGRATION:**

- Students record daily pronunciation drills

- AI analyzes pronunciation accuracy

- Provides score + specific feedback



---



# 10. TEACHER TRAINING: BECOMING A NET INSTRUCTOR



## 10.1 OVERVIEW



**NET teachers are not just English teachers. They are confidence coaches.**



### REQUIREMENTS



**HARD SKILLS:**

- Native or near-native English fluency (C1+)

- Understanding of Four Pillars

- Classroom management experience



**SOFT SKILLS:**

- High energy, enthusiasm

- Empathy for Vietnamese learners

- Ability to improvise and adapt



**DISQUALIFICATIONS:**

- Grammar-obsessed teachers (violates Pillar 4)

- Low-energy teachers (can't create flow)

- Judgmental teachers (creates Mouse Voice)



---



## 10.2 TRAINING PROGRAM (4 WEEKS)



### WEEK 1: PHILOSOPHY & PSYCHOLOGY



**Topics:**

- History of NET (founder story)

- Four Pillars deep dive

- Mouse→Tiger transformation theory

- Gospel 444 (branding, visual identity)



**Assessment:**

- Written exam on Four Pillars

- Teach 10-minute demo lesson (recorded)



---



### WEEK 2: LESSON DELIVERY



**Topics:**

- 10-5-5-3 structure

- Energy management

- Pairing strategies

- Time management



**Practice:**

- Shadow an experienced NET teacher

- Teach 30-minute segment (observed)

- Receive feedback



---



### WEEK 3: CRISIS MANAGEMENT



**Topics:**

- 10 emergency scripts

- Handling difficult students

- Tech troubleshooting

- Conflict resolution



**Practice:**

- Role-play 5 crisis scenarios

- Get evaluated on response



---



### WEEK 4: ASSESSMENT & SYSTEMS



**Topics:**

- Intake assessment process

- Progress check methodology

- Buddy system management

- NET PET integration



**Practice:**

- Conduct mock intake assessment

- Write progress report

- Pair two students as buddies



---



## 10.3 CERTIFICATION



**To become certified NET teacher:**

1. Pass 4-week training program

2. Teach 10 observed lessons (scored by senior teacher)

3. Achieve 85%+ satisfaction rating from students

4. Complete 20 hours of classroom time



**Certified teachers receive:**

- NET Teacher Certificate

- Access to full lesson library

- Teacher community Slack group

- Ongoing PD opportunities



---



## 10.4 ONGOING PROFESSIONAL DEVELOPMENT



**MONTHLY:**

- Teacher meetups (share best practices)

- Lesson plan reviews (peer feedback)

- Student success story sharing



**QUARTERLY:**

- Advanced training workshops (pronunciation, business English)

- New lesson content creation

- Classroom observations (peer-to-peer)



**ANNUALLY:**

- Recertification exam

- Teaching philosophy refresh

- New skill development (AI tools, gamification)

1. THE AXIOMS: THE IMMUTABLE LAWS
1.1 LAW OF INPUT (The 80/20 Rule)
Language is acquired, not memorized.

The Law: A student cannot produce output (speaking) without massive input (listening).

The Ratio: 80% Comprehensible Input / 20% Struggle.

The Violation: If a teacher explains a grammar rule for more than 2 minutes without examples, they are creating NOISE.

1.2 LAW OF VOLUME (Tiger Physics)
Volume is a proxy for confidence.

The Law: You cannot speak fluent English quietly. Mumbling covers mistakes; loudness exposes them.

The Mandate: "If I can't hear you, you didn't say it."

The Metric: Students must project to the back of the room.

1.3 LAW OF CONTEXT (Death to Lists)
Isolated words are dead data.

The Law: Never write a single word on the board without a sentence.

The Method: "Sarcastic" (Word) → "He is sarcastic" (Bad) → "Oh great, another meeting!" (Good).

The Drift: Teaching vocabulary lists = Immediate Fail.

1.4 LAW OF SAFETY (The Psychological Net)
Fear kills fluency.

The Law: The classroom is a "High Stakes / Low Risk" environment.

The Stakes: We demand effort and energy.

The Risk: We remove the shame of failure. A mistake is data, not a sin.

2. THE ARCHITECTURE: THE 10-5-5-3 MASTER PROTOCOL
2.1 THE STRUCTURE
Every 90-minute session is a constructed narrative.

SEGMENT	COMPONENT	DURATION	PURPOSE
0	The Spark	10 Min	Energy Injection / Warm-up Game
1	The Download	15 Min	10 Vocab + 5 Idioms (Input)
2	The Activation	15 Min	5 Slang + Guided Drills (Mimicry)
3	The Arena (I)	15 Min	Mid-Lesson Game (Gamified Practice)
4	The Flow	20 Min	Free Conversation / Roleplay
5	The Arena (II)	10 Min	Final Challenge (High Intensity)
6	The Mirror	5 Min	Feedback & "Tiger Wins"
2.2 THE CONTENT BLOCKS
10 CORE VOCABULARY (The Bricks)
Criteria: High-frequency, topic-specific words.

Ban: No obscure academic words (e.g., "Penultimate"). Use "Second to last."

5 IDIOMS (The Glue)
Why: Native speakers communicate in idioms. Without them, students sound like robots.

Example: Don't teach "To be expensive." Teach "It costs an arm and a leg."

5 SLANG / PHRASAL VERBS (The Flavor)
Why: This is how real people speak. It builds cultural confidence.

Example: "Ghosting," "Vibe check," "Burnout."

3 GAMES (The Engine)
Rule: Games are NOT rewards for working hard. Games ARE the work.

Placement: Start, Middle, End. No exceptions.

3. THE CURRICULUM TRACKS
3.1 TRACK A: ZERO-TO-HERO (General English)
For the everyday learner escaping Grammar Prison.

Phase 1: Survival (Weeks 1-4)

Topics: Ordering Food, Directions, Introductions, "Mouse Voice" Rehab.

Goal: Stop apologizing. Speak in full sentences.

Phase 2: Connection (Weeks 5-8)

Topics: Hobbies, Travel, Dating, Pop Culture, Sarcasm.

Goal: Make a joke in English. Understand a meme.

Phase 3: Opinion (Weeks 9-12)

Topics: Debates, Current Events, Psychology, Storytelling.

Goal: Argue a point without translating.

3.2 TRACK B: CORPORATE WARRIOR (Business English)
For the professional who needs ROI immediately.

Phase 1: The Daily Grind (Weeks 1-4)

Topics: Daily Standups, Email Etiquette, Zoom Small Talk, Explaining Delays.

Goal: Handle a 15-minute sync without sweating.

Phase 2: The Negotiation (Weeks 5-8)

Topics: Agreeing/Disagreeing politely, Giving Feedback, Asking for a Raise, Sales.

Goal: Push back on a client request politely.

Phase 3: The Boardroom (Weeks 9-12)

Topics: Presentations, Data storytelling, Leadership communication, Crisis management.

Goal: Deliver a 5-minute pitch to a foreign boss.

4. PSYCH-OPS: THE MOUSE-TO-TIGER TRANSFORMATION
4.1 THE DIAGNOSIS
You are not just a teacher; you are a Confidence Doctor.

SYMPTOM	DIAGNOSIS	PRESCRIPTION
Whispering answers	Mouse Voice	The "Back of Room" Drill (Force volume)
"Sorry, my English is bad"	Apology Reflex	"The Apology Tax" (Pay 10k VND per 'Sorry')
Silence during Q&A	Freeze Response	Pair Share (Talk to neighbor first)
Translating in head	Processing Lag	Speed Rounds (Forcing instinct over logic)
4.2 THE "TIGER SHIFT" PROTOCOLS
1. THE INTERRUPTION DOCTRINE
Traditional teachers never interrupt. NET Teachers interrupt to encourage.

Script: "STOP! Did you hear that? You just used the past tense perfectly! High five! Keep going!"

Why: Dopamine hit. Reinforces the win immediately.

2. THE IDENTITY HACK
We do not say "Student." We say "Speaker."

The Frame: "You are not learning English. You are an English Speaker who is practicing."

The Result: Shifts identity from "Subordinate" to "Practitioner."

3. THE ERROR CELEBRATION
When a student makes a spectacular mistake:

Teacher: "YES! I love that mistake! You took a risk! Who can tell me why that was a brave attempt?"

Result: Removes the stigma of being wrong.

5. THE ENERGY ECONOMY: CLASSROOM MANAGEMENT V2
5.1 THE ENERGY GRAPH
A lesson must look like a heartbeat, not a flatline.

Start: High Energy (Game)

Input: Focus Energy (Learning)

Practice: Buzz Energy (Chaos/Noise is Good)

Mid-Game: Spike Energy

Cool Down: Focus Energy

End: High Energy

5.2 STATE BREAKS
If you see "The Glazed Eye" (students zoning out), you must trigger a State Break immediately. Do not finish your sentence.

Physical: "Everyone stand up. Switch seats with someone wearing a different color."

Vocal: "Everyone take a deep breath. Scream 'AHHH' in 3, 2, 1."

Mental: "Stop English. 30 seconds of Vietnamese gossip. Go." (Then snap back to English).

5.3 BOARD ARCHITECTURE
The whiteboard is your UI. Do not clutter it.

Left Column: The Bank (New Vocab/Phrases). Stays up all class.

Center: The Workspace (Examples, Diagrams, Grammar Patterns). Erased frequently.

Right Column: The Graveyard (Common mistakes corrected). "Don't say X, say Y."

Colors:

Black/Blue: Information.

Red: Corrections/STOP signs.

Green/Purple: Emphasis/Magic words.

6. CRISIS PROTOCOLS: THE BLACK BOX
6.1 SCRIPT: THE MUTINY (Topic is too hard)
Situation: Class looks confused, frustrated, silence is heavy. Script:

"Pause. I'm looking at your faces, and I see I've made this too complicated. That's on me. Let's scrap this explanation. We're going to do it the simple way. Everyone close your books. Look at me." (Switch to simple analogy/visuals).

6.2 SCRIPT: THE TOXIC STUDENT (Mocking others)
Situation: Student A laughs at Student B's pronunciation meanly. Script:

(Stop class immediately. Dead silence. Serious face). "Hey. We don't do that here. In this room, mistakes are gold. If you laugh at a mistake, you kill the bravery in the room. Are we clear?" (Wait for nod. Return to high energy immediately).

6.3 SCRIPT: THE GRAMMAR LAWYER
Situation: Student keeps asking "Why?" about obscure grammar rules during speaking practice. Script:

"That's a great question for a linguist. But we aren't linguists, we are speakers. The rule is 'X'. For now, trust me and use it. We can dissect the 'Why' after class if you want, but right now, we need to flow."

7. AI INTEGRATION: THE CYBORG TEACHER
7.1 NET PET (The Tool)
NET PET (our custom AI) is the TA (Teaching Assistant). Use it.

7.2 IN-CLASS AI USE CASES
The Scenario Generator:

Teacher: "NET PET, give me a roleplay scenario involving a angry customer and a barista, using the words 'Sarcastic' and 'Refund'."

Result: Infinite, instant content generation.

The Pronunciation Judge:

Activity: Have a student speak a sentence into the NET PET app. Let the AI give the feedback score (removes teacher bias/pressure).

The Thesaurus:

Student: "How do I say 'very hungry'?"

Teacher: "Ask the AI. 'Give me 5 slang words for very hungry'."

8. PRONUNCIATION: THE MECHANICS OF SOUND
8.1 THE PHYSICAL APPROACH
Vietnamese students struggle because their mouth muscles aren't trained for English. Stop explaining "tongue position" and start using Physical Anchors.

8.2 THE "TH" ANCHOR
The Cue: "The Snake."

Action: Stick tongue out fully (exaggerated). Bite it gently. Blow air.

Drill: "Thirty-Three Thieves." Students must physically spit/spray. If it's not messy, it's wrong.

8.3 THE "ENDING SOUND" ANCHOR
The Problem: Vietnamese words don't have ending consonant sounds (e.g., "Rice" becomes "Rai").

The Fix: "The Click."

Action: Students must clap or snap their fingers on the last letter.

Drill: "Li-KE" (Snap). "Want-T" (Snap).

8.4 THE "INTONATION" ANCHOR
The Problem: Monotone (Robot voice).

The Fix: "The Conductor."

Action: Students use their hand to draw the sound wave in the air. High hand = High pitch. Low hand = Low pitch.

Drill: "RE-ally?" (Hand goes up). "Re-ALLY." (Hand goes down).

9. GAMIFICATION ENGINE: THE FUN MANDATE
9.1 HIGH ENERGY GAMES (Movement)
Hot Seat: Student sits with back to board. Class describes word behind them.

Board Race: Teams rush to write words/sentences on the board.

4-Corners: Teacher asks opinion ("I like Durian"). Students run to corners labeled "Agree," "Disagree," etc.

9.2 FOCUS GAMES (Mental)
Alibi: Two students are "suspects" and leave room to create a story. Class interrogates them separately to find holes in the lie. (Practices Past Tense).

Taboo: Describe a word without using forbidden keywords.

The Price is Right: Guess the cost of items (Practices numbers/currency).

9.3 FLOW GAMES (Speaking)
Speed Dating: Two lines. 1 minute conversation. Rotate.

Devil's Advocate: Teacher gives a controversial statement ("Coffee is bad"). Student MUST argue for it, even if they disagree.

Chain Story: Student A says one sentence. Student B adds one.

10. CORPORATE SPECIAL OPS: B2B TEACHING STANDARDS
10.1 THE EXECUTIVE PRESENCE
When teaching corporate clients (Samsung, Shopee, etc.), the "Fun Teacher" vibe must be dialed down, and the "Executive Coach" vibe dialed up.

Dress Code: Smart Casual / Professional. No t-shirts.

The Setup: Whiteboard must be structured. Handouts must be premium.

The Language: Use business context examples (KPIs, Q4, Deliverables).

10.2 THE ROI FOCUS
Every lesson must end with a "Monday Morning Application."

Teacher: "How will you use this idiom in your meeting next week?"

Teacher: "Rewrite your last email using the passive voice structure we just learned."

10.3 HIERARCHY MANAGEMENT
In Vietnam, junior staff may be afraid to speak in front of senior staff.

Protocol: Break hierarchies in pair work. Pair Manager with Manager, Junior with Junior initially.

Protocol: Or, create "English Names/Personas" to detach from corporate rank. "In this room, you are not the CEO. You are 'Tiger'."

Improve ultimate



Our Unique Approach: The Natural English Method



We believe that learning a new language should mirror how you mastered your first: naturally, enjoyably, and effectively.



Learn Like a Native: Our method is built on the science that shows we learn best by modeling how we learned as children. You start by listening to native speakers, then mimicking them, allowing speaking, pronunciation, grammar, reading, and writing to develop naturally over time.



Focus on Comprehensible Input: You'll listen to material that you already understand at least 60% of the time. This focus on listening for context helps you effortlessly grasp meaning and the situation, forming the foundation of fluency.



Break the Grammar Barrier: Unlike traditional schooling, we don't drill complex grammar rules. You learned to speak and communicate before you knew grammar, and with our training, it will come to you naturally, too.



Master the Sounds: We ditch complicated phonics for the effective Jolly Phonics system developed in England. We will help you master all 42 sounds and tackle the non-phonetic words in English.



Our Methods: Training & Coaching

We are more than trainers—we are your coaches and accountability partners, dedicated to integrating English into your life.



Active, Conversational Lessons: Our lessons are not lectures. You will be an active participant, trained to give effective presentations and to converse naturally and like a native speaker.



Relevant Vocabulary & Association: Forget the Duolingo approach. We introduce relevant vocabulary through full sentences and natural conversation. You learn by association—the proven way your brain makes language connections.



Prioritize Effective Expression: It’s not about how many words you know; it’s about knowing how to express yourself confidently and effectively with the basic vocabulary you already have.



The Power of Spaced Repetition (Add your specific explanation of this technique here to complete the idea.)



The NET Difference: Fun & Accountability

We turn the "chore" of studying into an engaging, daily habit.



Make it Fun, Not a Chore: Studies show we learn significantly more when we are having fun and engaging with the material. We help you make listening a part of your routine—using podcasts, YouTube, and other content tailored to your personal likes and dislikes.



Daily Home Study Program: We ensure you are doing your daily 1 hour of listening (45 minutes of fun, high-interest content and 15 minutes of speaking practice) that is tailored to fit seamlessly into your routine.



Safe Environment for Growth: We understand the reluctance to make mistakes. We create a safe environment where we challenge our students to push past the fear of speaking English, helping you learn to speak confidently.



Added program value-



Buddy system - We will pair you up with a partner each week where you will add 1 hour of practice to your week, where you will review and practically apply through natural conversation what we have been learning in our trainings.
